On September 3, 1989, after the United States and the Soviet Union declared the end of the Cold War and the beginning of a new era of cooperation for lasting world peace, democratization and market economic systems have spread throughout Eastern Europe and the Third World, and positive changes have occurred, such as resolving disputes between countries through dialogue and negotiation. However, potential conflict factors and new conflict factors that were latent during the Cold War have emerged and continue to threaten global security

[21] In other words, conflicts over ethnicity, territory, religion, etc. are rampant, and weapons of mass destruction, such as nuclear and chemical weapons, are being proliferated by wealthy countries (31816 0【『 00ㅁ06727) despite international disarmament efforts

[22] In addition, various transnational threats such as environmental pollution, terrorism, and refugee issues are on the rise

[23] The United States, a superpower, seeks world peace and stability through bilateral alliances and collective security organizations such as the North Atlantic Treaty Organization (NATO

[24] Although countries such as Russia and China may maintain different positions from the United States in resolving international issues, the United States' role in stabilizing the world order is expected to continue

[25] In addition, the UN's role in maintaining world peace is expanding

[26] The United Nations is promoting various negotiations and treaties for the reduction of strategic and conventional weapons, as well as their thorough implementation, and is performing practical peacekeeping functions through peacekeeping operations

[27] Section 2 New Threats and Challenges Diversification of Security Concepts and Spread of Transnational Threats In the post-Cold War world, the factors of conflict have diversified into ethnic, territorial, religious, resource, and environmental issues, and the importance of economy and technology has become more prominent. As a result, the pursuit of national interests and securing international competitiveness have become key elements for national survival and prosperity. Accordingly, the traditional security concept centered on politics and the military has expanded into a comprehensive security concept that includes non-military aspects

[28] non-military aspects such as trade, finance, technology, information, environment, welfare, and culture are becoming the country's main security objectives

[29] Accordingly, in recent years, various threat factors such as economic crisis, environmental pollution, natural disasters, drugs, organized crime, and computer crime, as well as external military risks, are spreading as transnational threats that threaten not only individuals but also groups, countries, and even the entire world

[30] As future warfare evolves into a high-tech warfare, countries around the world are focusing on improving their military science and technology capabilities while also striving to develop systems that can effectively operate advanced weapons

[31] In particular, major advanced military countries are focusing on developing information warfare (01010008010ㅁ0 '\81[876) capabilities to protect their own intelligence collection, production, and dissemination capabilities and to destroy or neutralize such capabilities of a hypothetical enemy country in preparation for the future war being developed into cyber warfare (29006 82000) and advanced 0419# warfare, and are spurring long-range ultra-precision strike capabilities, 0418 capabilities that allow commanders to directly check the battlefield situation in real time and direct missions, and furthermore, the military utilization of space

[32] In addition, they are working to prevent the spread of weapons of mass destruction, such as biological and biological weapons, and missiles, which are their means of delivery, while preparing for the development of military science and technology and changes in new war patterns, in order to suppress the spread of large scale warfare

[33] However, some countries are pursuing the development and production of nuclear weapons, posing a serious threat to international peace and stability

[34] India and Pakistan conducted nuclear and medium-range missile tests in competition again in April 1999, following the one in May

[35] In addition, so-called countries of concern, such as North Korea, Iran, Iraq, and Syria, are continuously pursuing the strengthening of their nuclear and long-range ballistic missile capabilities, which is heightening the international community's anxiety. As such, many countries have reached the level of technology to develop nuclear weapons and missiles, and the components necessary for development are also easily obtained through international black market transactions

[36] Furthermore, because biochemical weapons are easy to manufacture and inexpensive to produce, they can be easily possessed not only by many countries but also by criminal and terrorist groups, posing a major threat to global security

[37] Regional Disputes and Conflicts Today, conflicts in each region of the world are caused by a complex interaction of various factors. -18- --- Page 19 --- | Therefore, for at least a year, the Kusovo conflict' has been continuing in developed countries as a 'Southeast Asian civil war with no end in sight

[38] The issue escalated into an international issue due to the military intervention of the G20, and in the process, differences of opinion arose between the West and former socialist countries, including Russia and China

[39] However, it is expected that there will be a conflict between ethnic groups

[40] In addition to the third Russia-Czech Republic crisis, the region has also been experiencing a transition process since the end of the Cold War, with the Georgia Abkhaz conflict, Timor Leste in the former Soviet Union, and 13 other conflicts that have taken place in the region

[41] In particular, in the former Yugoslavia region, the international community is experiencing many difficulties in preventing and resolving conflicts due to domestic political and social conflicts, such as cultural antagonisms arising from diverse ethnic compositions, and complex international support relationships. As of the end of 1999, 83 conflicts were ongoing (see Appendix 1), and 18 of these conflicts are assessed to have resulted in more than 1,000 deaths

[42] Even in the year 3000, bloody conflicts continue in 45 conflict zones around the world, including the Sierra Leone Civil War and the Democratic Republic of the Congo Civil War

[43] In 1999, 9 out of 10 European countries were in conflict, and most of them were in the former socialist bloc countries of Europe. On June 10, 1999, Serbia accepted the UN's 'Kosovo Peace Plan', and in July, it signed the Western-led Comprehensive Peace Agreement and began to work towards establishing peace in Kosovo. The Kosovo conflict, which was caused by this, will take some time to be fundamentally resolved, and historical animosity and confrontation between ethnic groups, such as civil wars, still exist

[44] Regional conflicts are deeply rooted in colonial heritage, religion, and separatist movements, with the former and Afghan civil wars being prime examples

[45] Indonesia has been influencing separatist movements in East Timor, and in the Philippines, rebel activity has been on the rise recently due to the influence of national independence

[46] Declaration of Hot of Southwest Asia Region, India and Pakistan' by Jetek

[47] However, in 1999, during the Cargill incident, the first shellfish > 0 (also 6 packs ㅇㅇ 00 high

[48] Woo Min Pu Umtae Aete|9 Pu Se has three L-shaped trust zones and a mutual prevention of armed conflict between the two countries

[49] In addition, with regard to the Tamil independence movement, Sri Lanka has not found a clue to the solution

[50] In the Middle East, while diplomatic efforts to resolve the |> Dorothy Silver Price: Bu 90 Gume issue with Israel continue, terrorist attacks by dissatisfied forces continue unabated

[51] In addition, the conflict between Iraq and the West continues due to UN economic sanctions and inspections of weapons of mass destruction, and Iran has raised international concerns by demonstrating long-range ballistic missiles. Iran is involved in conflicts in the region, where about one-third (27) of all conflicts in the world are unfolding

[52] About 70-80% of the conflicts in the region arose during the struggle for power between political factions or ethnic groups and tribes after independence from Western powers in the 1960s

[53] It is also the place with the most conflicts in the world, including the Sierra Leone Civil War and the Democratic Republic of the Congo Civil War

[54] Meanwhile, four conflicts are ongoing in the Americas, but overall, the atmosphere of peace is spreading

[55] The Mexican Civil War, which had no notable armed conflicts during 1999, and the Peruvian civil war, where rebel forces were rapidly weakened by the government's active response, are assessed to have been reduced to the level of riots or terrorism

[56] However, the Colombian Civil War is considered the largest conflict zone in the Americas, as it is an extreme confrontation between left-wing forces linked to drug cartels and the right-wing government, resulting in many casualties

[57] Launched in 1945, the United Nations was not effective in maintaining international peace and security during the Cold War due to ideological conflicts among member states

[58] However, with the restoration of the UN's authority after the end of the Cold War, its role in international peace is increasing

[59] In particular, peacekeeping activities are actively underway, and the scope of activities includes conflict prevention, creation of an environment favorable to the establishment of a peace phenomenon, comprehensive monitoring of the implementation of agreements, and humanitarian relief supplies

[60] It is becoming more diverse, including enforcement activities

[61] Disarmament activities are also being promoted through the UN, focusing on the development, production, and international transfer of weapons of mass destruction

[62] We have achieved results through the extension of the Treaty on the Non-Proliferation of Nuclear Weapons through development and production control, strengthening the international cooperation system for the Missile Technology Control System, entry into force of the Chemical Weapons Convention, and negotiation of the Verification Protocol of the Biological Weapons Convention

[63] In order to control international transfers, he led the formation of various dispatch systems, such as the Wassenaar Arrangement and the Missile Technology Control System, to block the supply of goods, technology, and equipment directly or indirectly related to the production of weapons of mass destruction

[64] However, the UN has recently been facing difficulties due to financial difficulties and institutional problems in the Security Council

[65] While major developed countries, including the United States, are attempting to reform the UN's finances and reduce their share of the budget, Japan and Germany are demanding permanent membership in the Security Council as part of securing status within the UN commensurate with their financial contribution

[66] Many member states agree that the Security Council needs to be expanded and reorganized to reflect the new international reality. However, the scope of the Security Council expansion and the granting of veto power are not being agreed upon among member states

[67] In order to reduce strategic and conventional arms, the United States and Russia signed the First and Second Strategic Arms Reduction Agreements (START I), and the North Atlantic Treaty Organization (NATO) and the former Warsaw Pact (CFE) signed the Conventional Arms Reduction Treaty in Europe (INF

[217] The guided missile 2-6 1 46100 of the S Bokhan is equipped with 2 to 4 anti-ship 81" missiles, and has the ability to engage large friendly ships with guided missiles, making it very threatening

[218] The 50 or so submarines, mainly Romeo-class and Shark-class, have the ability to disrupt maritime traffic routes, attack surface ships, lay mines, and support the infiltration of special warfare units in the waters around the Korean Peninsula

[219] The support ships are comprised of landing ships, high-speed landing craft, and air units, and these ships are building and operating about 140 air-cushioned craft that can embark troops and conduct surprise landings at target areas

[220] Since hovercraft can maneuver even on flat ground, they can dock in most areas of the east and west coasts, and because they have the ability to maneuver at high speeds of over 50 knots, they can be used for simultaneous surprise landings in the early stages of a war

[221] In addition, Silk Wall (811) surface-to-ship missiles with a range of 95km are deployed on the east and west coasts, and the Silk Wall missiles deployed forward are capable of anti-ship attacks on Deokjeok Island in the West Sea and Sokcho and Yangyang in the East Sea

[222] The North Korean Air Force is composed of six aviation divisions, including three fighter/bomber divisions, two support aviation divisions, and one training aviation division, under the central control of the Air Force Command, and one helicopter brigade. The aircraft possession types are shown in Table 1-9 and Table 1-9. The North Korean Air Force's aircraft possession status is approximately 790 aircraft, approximately 80 aircraft, and approximately 520 aircraft (44-22 | Pohang | approximately 320 aircraft). The aircraft force consists of older fighter/bombers such as the 1414-15/17 and 11-28, which account for more than 45%, while the 1414-19/21 is the main model and has a quantitative advantage, and some new fighters such as the 14104-23/29 and 80-95 are also in possession

[223] In 1999, about 40 Mi-21 helicopters were introduced from Kazakhstan and several Mi-8 helicopters from Russia

[224] In line with the step-by-step promotion of the aviation industry, some parts are produced in-house, and in the early 1990s, with technical support from Russia, the 8410-29 new fighter was assembled and produced

[225] The North Korean Air Force has two air force sniper brigades, and approximately 300 AN-2 aircraft have the ability to infiltrate special forces deep into the rear through low-altitude, low-speed flight in case of emergency

[226] In addition, about 40% of fighters are deployed at forward bases, giving us the ability to launch surprise attacks across the country in a short period of time. In addition to the 20 or so operational bases, we have deployed about 70 air bases, including reserve and emergency runways, in forward and rear defense zones to ensure survivability in times of war

[227] Meanwhile, North Korea is building a powerful 16-29 fighter network by densely deploying early warning and air defense surveillance radars, low-altitude anti-aircraft guns, and medium- and high-altitude surface-to-air missiles throughout North Korea, including the Tongyeong 5 and [Su0 deep-sea facilities

[228] North Korea, with its entire society as a gigantic barracks system, has been strongly pursuing the four major military lines since the early 1960s, arming all its people and thus possessing a large reserve force, and has a mobilization system that can mobilize this force in a short period of time

[229] The military currently has a reserve force of approximately 7.48 million people, targeting mobilization of approximately 30% of the entire population aged 14 to 60, and conducts training for 15 to 80 days per year

[230] The composition of North Korea's reserve forces is approximately 1.73 million combat-mobilized Reserve Forces (males: 17-45 years old, females: 17-80 years old), 4.14 million Worker-Peasant Red Guards (mainly males: 46-60 years old), 1.18 million Red Youth Guards (aged 14-16, 4th-6th grades), equivalent to a high school military organization, and approximately 480,000 paramilitary units including the Ministry of People's Security and the Ministry of National Defense, the General Bureau of Military Mobilization, and the Speed Battle Youth Shock Brigade Rear Guard Command

[231] In terms of the mobilization system, the training corps directly controlled by the Ministry of the People's Armed Forces can be immediately mobilized and deployed into battle by unit, and the Red Youth Guard and Worker-Peasant Red Guard controlled by the Party's Civil Defense Department are also organized so that they can be mobilized within a short period of time according to the Party's instructions

[232] In particular, due to the nature of the North Korean system that controls the residents' relocation, job assignment, and travel, the reserve force is in a state of "possibility"; North Korea's level of strategic weapons development appears to be such that it has been seeking to develop nuclear weapons since the 1950s with technical support from the former Soviet Union

[233] North Korea possesses high-quality uranium mines with recoverable reserves of 4 million tons. --- Page 44 --- After building a large-scale nuclear complex in Yongbyon in the 1960s, it introduced research reactors from the former Soviet Union and trained nuclear experts, while accumulating

[234] In the 1970s, through intensive research on a series of nuclear fuel cycles, namely fuel refining, conversion, and processing technologies, the company succeeded in expanding the output of its own research reactor, and in 1980, it began construction of the 5 MW-class research reactor (Reactor

[235] In the 1980s, the focus was on the practical application of nuclear power and the completion of the nuclear development system, and in 1986, the operation of a uranium refining and conversion facility began. In 1989, construction of a 200 MW-class nuclear power plant was started in Taechon, and construction of a large-scale reprocessing facility and high explosive room began in Yongbyon

[236] It is believed that the nuclear fuel cycle, from securing nuclear fuel to reprocessing, was completed in the 1990s

[237] However, it is not certain whether nuclear weapons will be completed and possessed due to the problem of developing detonators and carriers that require advanced nuclear technology

[238] However, considering North Korea's ability to extract plutonium, the raw material for making nuclear weapons, it is estimated that North Korea possesses the ability to produce one or two rudimentary nuclear weapons

[239] In order to prevent North Korea from developing nuclear weapons, the United States agreed in Geneva in October 1994 to build two light-water reactors in exchange for freezing North Korea's nuclear weapons program and to supply 500,000 tons of heavy oil annually until the first light-water reactor was completed

[240] Accordingly, in September 1995, the Korean Peninsula Energy Development 7th District and North Korea signed a light-water reactor financing agreement, and construction of the light-water reactor is currently underway

[241] However, North Korea has been refusing to allow inspections of undeclared facilities and to collect samples from the 5MW reactor operation records even after announcing its acceptance of temporary and general inspections by the International Atomic Energy Agency in January

[242] Meanwhile, in April 2000, the sealing work for the spent fuel of reactor 504 was completed

[243] The Geumchang-ri area, where nuclear-related underground facilities had been suspected since mid-1998, was confirmed to have nuclear-related facilities through on-site inspections conducted by the United States in May 1999 and May

[244] Chemical and Biological Weapons North Korea recognized the importance of chemical warfare in late 1961 and began developing weapons, including establishing research and production facilities, in accordance with Kim Il-sung's "Chemicalization Declaration

[245] After that, starting in the 1980s, the country focused on developing chemical and biological weapons, following Kim Il-sung's teaching that "it is effective to produce poisonous and biological weapons and use them in combat

[246] Currently, North Korea is storing toxic agents such as nerve-wracking, blistering, blood-borne, emetic, and tear-jerking agents produced at eight chemical factories in six facilities, and the stockpile is estimated to be approximately 2,500 to 5,000 tons

[247] It is also believed to have the ability to cultivate and produce biological weapons such as anthrax

[248] In addition, North Korea possesses various chemical warfare weapons, and can launch chemical warfare attacks simultaneously in the frontline areas using mortars, field guns, and rockets with a caliber of 100 mm or more, and in the rear areas using missiles, fighters, bombers, and aircraft. In addition, there is a possibility that special forces can be used to secretly spread biological weapons in the rear areas

[249] They have organized chemical platoons up to the 1st level and are conducting chemical, biological, and radiological warfare training for not only soldiers but also paramilitary units and civilians. These chemical and biological weapons are inexpensive to produce, making them economical and efficient, and they are easy to destroy evidence. Therefore, despite the global trend toward banning them, North Korea, which is facing serious economic difficulties, will likely try to maintain this capability

[250] Medium and long-range weapons North Korea has been developing ballistic missiles since the early 1980s and has already produced and operationally deployed the 80012-ㅇ, which is an improved version of the Soviet-made 8001- and has a range of 500 ㅠ

[251] In 1993, the Nodong-1 missile with a range of approximately 1300 km was test-launched and operationally deployed in 1997. In August 1998, an attempt was made to place a small satellite into orbit using a modified Taepodong-1 missile carrier, but this attempt failed

[252] However, considering that the various functions, including engine combustion of the carrier and multi-stage separation of the projectile, were performed without any problems, it is believed that North Korea possesses the capability to develop medium- and long-range missiles

[253] The maximum ranges of the Taepodong 1 and 2 long-range ballistic missiles currently under development by North Korea are estimated to be 3,000 to 2,500 meters and 6,700 meters, respectively

[254] Such ballistic missile capabilities pose a threat not only to the Korean Peninsula but also to countries in Northeast Asia, and North Korea is facing international criticism for exporting its own missiles to the Middle East and Southwest Asia

[255] Additionally, missile talks have been ongoing between the US and North Korea since 1996 on issues such as joining the "Missile Technology Control System (MTCR)" and suspending exports and production, but no agreement has been reached yet

[256] Since the launch of the Daepodong missile in August 1998, a comprehensive missile agreement has been pursued between the United States and North Korea

[257] Meanwhile, on September 24, 1999, the North Korean Ministry of Foreign Affairs announced that it would suspend missile launches during the US-North Korea high-level talks, and on October 12, 2000, in the ‘US-North Korea Joint Communique’ agreed upon during the visit to the US by First Vice Chairman of the National Defense Commission Jo Myong-rok, it reaffirmed the suspension of missile launches during the missile talks

[258] It is believed that North Korea's purpose in producing and possessing medium- and long-range guided weapons along with chemical and biological weapons is to use them as a means of responding to and threatening the expansion of military influence by the United States and the Republic of Korea, while also using them as a card for external negotiations to ensure the survival of the regime

[259] In addition, this weapon type of can play a decisive role in operational and tactical situations

[260] That is, by using this, they are trying to realize their military strategy of short-term, rapid action by striking major cities and strategic targets in the front and rear, including the capital area of Korea

[302] Our military has set national defense goals to achieve national security, and has established basic concepts for national defense development in consideration of the importance of achieving these goals

[303] Based on these basic concepts of national defense development, the national defense policy direction is established to achieve consistent and efficient national defense goals. National defense goals: Protect the nation from external military threats and invasion, support peaceful unification, and contribute to regional stability and world peace. First, 'Protect the nation from external military threats and invasion

[313] The development of the ROK-US alliance and the strengthening of security cooperation with neighboring countries have contributed greatly to preventing the recurrence of war on the Korean Peninsula, economic development, and laying the foundation for independent national defense over the past half-century

[349] Since the historic inter-Korean summit in June 2000, North Korea has shown some changes, such as refraining from slandering South Korea in its broadcasts. However, the North Korean military's military capabilities remain unchanged, as it continues to increase training levels, strengthen its military power, and reinforce its combat readiness posture

[350] ROK Military Situation Accordingly, our military is maintaining a strong military readiness posture to be able to immediately respond to any threat or provocation from North Korea, and is establishing a solid joint defense system to prevent tensions and instability from developing on the Korean Peninsula, while also engaging in various military diplomacy

[351] Maintaining the Early Warning System and Crisis Management System Our military operates the ROK-US joint surveillance assets to monitor the activities of the North Korean military 24 hours a day

[352] In the frontline area, the North Korean ground forces are monitored using soldiers' visual surveillance, radar, thermal imaging equipment, and electronic warfare equipment, and at sea, the maritime activities of the North Korean military are monitored in real time using automated surveillance equipment such as maritime surveillance radar and the Navy Tactical Command and Control System (C4I

[353] In the air, North Korea's aerial activities are monitored and tracked through 24-hour airspace surveillance centered around the Central Air Defense Control Center, and support is also received from U.S. military intelligence satellites when necessary

[354] In the event that any unusual signs of North Korean military activity are detected through these precise surveillance activities, the ROK-US joint crisis management and cooperation system is also being maintained firmly to respond more effectively

[355] The development of the ROK-US joint crisis management system began with the signing of a related agreement in 1994 and a detailed implementation memorandum of understanding in 1995. In 1996, a memorandum of understanding was signed to further develop the joint crisis management procedures, and in 1999, a crisis response system capable of consistent crisis response from the same perspective was established

[356] In addition, through regular and ad hoc joint crisis response exercises held four times a year by the ROK-US alliance, we ensure systematic measures by the ROK-US alliance in the event of a crisis by fostering mutual intelligence exchange and proficiency in a series of procedures

[357] In addition, the Joint Chiefs of Staff establishes all procedures based on the crisis response regulations, and accordingly, designates and organizes necessary personnel as crisis response personnel in advance and cultivates practical crisis response capabilities through surprise call-up training during peacetime

[358] In addition, in order to quickly grasp the situation and take action, we are establishing the operation of a high-speed command post and command post automation system, and in case of an emergency, we are securing emergency [bo epi wave means] so that the Joint Chiefs of Staff and all operational Joint Chiefs of Staff can immediately activate the crisis response system

[359] Peacetime Military Readiness Posture Our military maintains a perfect military readiness posture, including establishing a joint operational posture of the Army, Navy, and Air Force, so that it can defend the country, protect the lives and property of the people, and further contribute to the maintenance of international peace under any circumstances

[360] On land, North and South Korea are installing barbed wire fences and operating surveillance posts along the 250-odd military demarcation lines where they stand face to face, and are carrying out surveillance missions along the coastline

[361] In addition, we are conducting surveillance activities with naval vessels, submarines, and aircraft in our maritime jurisdiction areas including the five northwestern islands and the east, west, and south coasts, including Marado, Ulleungdo, and Dokdo

[362] In the air, high-performance air force aircraft conduct day and night surveillance flights, and use various radars and cutting-edge equipment to monitor and control all aircraft activities entering the Korean Air Defense Identification Zone (KADIZ

[363] In addition, in inland areas, an integrated civil-military defense system is established to prepare for various provocations such as infiltration/terrorism by the North Korean military, and all/any of the military units, police, coast guard, reserve forces, defense forces, fisheries departments, local governments, etc. are dispatched upon residents' report

[364] We are building a comprehensive performance system

[382] The exercise was conducted to prepare for the infiltration of North Korea's special forces into the rear area, and mainly included bilateral field maneuver training between brigades and joint landing operation exercises

[384] Rim of the Pacific Exercise (RIMPAC Exercise) is a multinational joint maritime comprehensive maneuver exercise conducted every other year (even years) under the leadership of the U.S. 3rd Fleet since 1977 with the participation of seven countries, including Korea, the United States, Australia, Canada, Chile, the United Kingdom, and Japan

[385] The exercise is conducted to secure the safety of maritime traffic routes in the Pacific Ocean between the navies of Pacific coastal countries and to enhance joint response capabilities in the event of a maritime dispute. [387] Team Spirit Exercise is a joint ROK-U.S. field maneuver exercise that has been conducted annually since 1976 to deter North Korea's war ambitions on the Korean Peninsula and strengthen the ROK-U.S. security cooperation system

[388] This exercise is essentially a regular joint exercise between allies and is purely defensive in nature

[389] North Korea has previously criticized this exercise as an attack drill for an invasion, but this is not true, and we have also offered to North Korea to observe the Team Spirit exercise

[390] This exercise is essential for confirming the ROK-U.S. alliance and developing joint operations and military capabilities for the defense of South Korea, but has been suspended since 1994 to improve inter-Korean relations

[391] The training exercise is a Korean military self-command post that has been conducted under the control of the Joint Chiefs of Staff since 1996. In preparation for war, the Joint Chiefs of Staff and the Operations Command take the lead and conduct it every May to familiarize themselves with the war conduct procedures and improve operational command capabilities. During the training exercise, the command of the Joint Chiefs of Staff and the Operations Command participate in the exercise as an exercise execution team, and other organizations form response teams and acting teams and conduct exercises using war game models at the unit command post and combat simulation headquarters

[392] Through the Yalu River exercise, the Joint Chiefs of Staff mastered operational command and staff activity procedures and wartime transition procedures, and implemented operational plans for each unit, developed procedures for restoring wartime combat power and a system for calculating and supporting wartime support requirements. In particular, starting with the 1999 exercise, the results of the exercise were improved by linking and operating the command post automation system

[393] The Hoguk Training is a large-scale joint training exercise that has been conducted since 1996 under the supervision of the Joint Chiefs of Staff, focusing on the implementation of operational plans by the Army, Navy, and Air Force along coastal and inland axes

[394] The 2000 Hoguk training was conducted along the east coast and central inland axis with the participation of the Army, Navy, Air Force, and Marine Corps. Through this training, the requirements for combat development by function were derived and supplemented, and the training was boldly delegated to the National Defense Council, creating an atmosphere of autonomous and creative responsibility training

[395] In addition, we are establishing a comprehensive development plan for training centers and promoting the installation of integrated training centers by region and area, and in particular, we are in the process of installing a scientific combat training center that can conduct training similar to real-life combat using specialized military units and MILES equipment, and scientifically collect and analyze training results to derive lessons

[396] The Navy is improving its combat capabilities by conducting various naval component operations, including anti-ship warfare, anti-submarine warfare, landing warfare, mine warfare, rescue warfare, and special warfare

[397] Ships of the class above the ship are focusing on anti-submarine warfare, anti-air warfare, and electronic warfare training, and the mother combat ship is focusing on three-dimensional follow-up training centered on regular warfare to suppress large-scale maritime provocations by the enemy, and is conducting training centered on offensive and defensive warfare to improve its actual combat capabilities

[398] Landing warfare ships are improving their landing operation capabilities through landing assault and landing surprise training, and mine warfare ships are cultivating mine-laying capabilities to effectively respond to enemy mine-laying

[399] The submarine force is focusing on improving its special warfare capabilities through coastal reconnaissance, surprise strike, and survival training, while conducting realistic training such as port blockades, anti-submarine warfare, surface force surveillance, and torpedo launching training

[400] The Naval Aviation Unit is focusing on improving its three-dimensional operational capabilities by conducting maritime surveillance operations and anti-submarine warfare training in cooperation with ships, as well as training to guide anti-ship missile attacks on targets located outside radar detection range

[401] In particular, since 1998, the Navy has been conducting large-scale maritime comprehensive maneuver training annually with the participation of all available forces of the Navy and units of the Army and Air Force, thereby developing the ability to conduct joint operations through multi-ship warfare under multiple threat situations

[402] In addition, we are strengthening the three-dimensional linkage of surface, underwater, and air power to effectively repel the infiltration of special operation forces using the enemy's high-speed landing force

[403] In order to build a combined maritime defense posture, the Navy is actively conducting joint military readiness training with the navies of allied countries, including the United States (조빅, 단 10 `니 타 ^ 8 부 44 」| ire --- Page 73 --- and is also continuously developing the combined operational system between the ROK and US navies by strengthening the existing ROK-US joint training

[404] Meanwhile, we are expanding military training and exchange of training observation groups with neighboring countries, and by conducting joint training in parallel with our warships when they visit foreign countries, such as during the Naval Academy cadet cruise training, and when foreign warships visit Korea, we are enhancing our Navy's operational capabilities and raising our international status

[405] The Marine Corps focuses on joint and independent landing training as part of the amphibious force, while the Army, Air Force, and Navy conduct maritime maneuver training [fun tti0 ae1 The Air Force conducts all training assuming actual battlefields and combat situations so that it can complete the given mission under any conditions

For ground training, we are strengthening tactical shooting after mid and low-altitude infiltration and night and all-weather strikes to deliver lethal blows to enemy strategic facilities and major military forces

[408] The legal and institutional basis for the current peace on the Korean Peninsula is the Armistice Agreement concluded in 1953, and in the Basic Agreement between the South and the North (Article 5) concluded in 1992, the South and the North agreed to “make efforts to transform the current armistice into a solid state of peace and to maintain the current armistice agreement until a state of peace is established,” thereby reaffirming this

[409] The government maintains the position that the existing armistice agreement must be observed until a solid peace is established on the Korean Peninsula, and is making great efforts to establish a solid peace in the Korean Peninsula

[410] On the other hand, we are seriously considering a plan to convert the armistice agreement into a peace agreement by promoting four-party talks. The four-party talks are intended to establish a peace regime on the Korean Peninsula in a way that the two Koreas, the actual parties to peace on the Korean Peninsula, take the lead and are supported by the United States and China

[411] Meanwhile, the government is continuously pursuing the path of reconciliation and cooperation in maintaining relations with North Korea, recognizing that North Korea's escape from international isolation and becoming a responsible member of the international community will contribute to the stability of the Korean Peninsula

[412] On August 9, 2000, the government declared its will to implement four major tasks, including supporting the recovery of the North Korean economy through the 'Mecklenburg-Vorpommern Declaration', ending the Cold War on the Korean Peninsula and pursuing a peace policy, resolving the issue of separated families, and resuming dialogue between North Korean authorities, both domestically and internationally. As a result, the government held a historic inter-Korean summit in Pyongyang from June 18 to 15, 2000, and achieved the adoption of the 'June 15 Inter-Korean Joint Declaration

[413] Since the inter-Korean summit, both sides have been actively promoting various exchange and cooperation projects between the South and the North through inter-Korean summits and ministerial talks to faithfully implement the '6/15 Inter-Korean Joint Delineation

[414] Meanwhile, the Ministry of National Defense held the 1st Inter-Korean Defense Ministers' Meeting from September 8 to 9, thereby militarily preparing for the implementation of the 'June 15 South-North Joint Line' agreed upon by the leaders of the South and the North

[415] In addition, the Ministry of National Defense will provide military support for the Gyeongui Line railway and road connection project agreed upon by both South and North Korea at the 8th Inter-Korean Ministerial Talks. Section 1: Maintaining the Korean Peninsula Peace Regime, the Armistice Regime, and General-Level Talks at Panmunjom Currently, peace on the Korean Peninsula is based on the armistice regime

[416] The armistice system is a system maintained in accordance with the Armistice Agreement signed in 1953, and has functioned as a general and institutional device for preventing armed conflict and managing crises on the Korean Peninsula for the past half century

[417] However, North Korea refused to participate in the plenary session of the Military Armistice Commission after the UN Command appointed a South Korean general as the senior representative to the Commission in March 1991, preventing the Commission from functioning normally. Even after agreeing to abide by the current armistice agreement until a solid state of peace was established on the Korean Peninsula in the "Basic Agreement between the South and the North," which came into effect in 1999, North Korea refused to activate the armistice system, including by withdrawing the North Korean and Chinese Military Armistice Commission and the Neutral Nations Supervisory Commission, and in March 1995 proposed holding general-level talks between the United States and North Korea in place of the Military Armistice Commission

[418] However, the United Nations Command and the Ministry of National Defense of the Republic of Korea, judging that it was necessary to maintain a responsible dialogue channel for crisis management within the armistice system, proposed holding general-level talks between the United Nations Command and the North Korean military in September 1998, and North Korea responded, leading to 11 rounds of talks held from June 1998 to September

[419] The general-level talks between the UN Command and the North Korean military are talks that deal with the implementation of the armistice agreement. Four representatives from the Military Armistice Commission are participating from the UN Command side, and each representative exercises equal speaking rights

[420] This meeting has served as a crisis management channel to determine the causes and responsibilities of North Korea's violations of the Armistice Agreement and to prevent the escalation of conflict

[421] However, when the Battle of Yeonpyeong broke out in June 1999, North Korea began to insist on holding working-level talks between the US and North Korea to address the issue of the West Sea maritime border, and began to take the bold approach of not responding to any contact at Panmunjom unless the UN Command responded to them

[422] Additionally, North Korea is insisting that the United States directly take over the remains of American soldiers killed in the Korean War, which were being delivered to the United Nations Command at Panmunjom in accordance with the provisions of the Armistice Agreement and existing practices

[423] In this way, North Korea is denying the existence of the UN Command by excluding it, which is responsible for the actual implementation of the Armistice Agreement, from dialogue and contact partners, and is seeking to conclude a peace treaty between the US and North Korea through direct contact with the US

[424] However, the Ministry of National Defense maintains its position that the current Armistice Agreement must continue to be observed until a solid state of peace is established on the Korean Peninsula in accordance with the provisions of the Armistice Agreement and the Basic Agreement between the South and the North, and that the Military Armistice Commission, the general-level talks held within its framework, and the Neutral Nations Supervisory Commission must maintain their normal functions

[425] Article 60 of the Armistice Agreement stipulates that “the governments of the two sides shall, within three months after the Armistice Agreement is signed and becomes effective, dispatch representatives to convene a political conference to discuss the peaceful resolution of the Korean question.”

[426] In accordance with this provision, the two sides held preliminary talks for a political conference in October 1953, but they broke down. In 1954, 30 countries involved in the Korean War gathered in Geneva to hold talks, but they ended without any significant results, and the armistice system has remained unstable since then

[427] Meanwhile, North Korea continued to insist on the establishment of a peace regime between North and South Korea, and from 1974 onwards, it began to demand the conclusion of a peace agreement between the US and North Korea

[428] In particular, as the crisis deepened due to North Korea's pacifist issue after 1993, North Korea, under the pretext of establishing a 'new peace guarantee system', signed a peace agreement with the US in April 1994, and on April 10, 1996, jointly proposed holding four-party talks involving South and North Korea, the US, and China to 'establish a permanent peace system on the Korean Peninsula'. The four-party talks began to demand the resumption of the Korean War

[429] This refers to a peace plan that seeks to resolve the peace issue on the Korean Peninsula in a way that is agreed upon by the two Koreas, the principal parties to the ROK-US agreement, and supported by the US and China, the relevant countries

[430] As North Korea expressed its intention to accept the four-party talks proposal by South Korea and the United States, a joint briefing session for the four-party talks was held in early 1997, and at the third preliminary meeting in November of that year, it was agreed that the agenda for the four-party talks would be a single agenda of “various issues for establishing a peace regime and easing tensions on the Korean Peninsula,” and that the first meeting would be held in Geneva in December 1997 and December 1998 accordingly

[431] Two rounds of talks were held in March, but no concrete implications were reached due to differences in the positions of South Korea, the United States, and North Korea

[432] That is, while South Korea and the United States proposed forming a subcommittee to discuss issues of establishing a peace regime and easing tensions and building trust, North Korea maintained its position that the withdrawal of US troops from South Korea and the conclusion of a peace treaty between North Korea and the US should be guaranteed to be discussed and negotiated as a priority

[433] However, at the third meeting held in October 1998, the participants agreed to form the "Subcommittee on Tension Easing" and the "Subcommittee on Peace System Establishment," and at the fourth meeting held in January 1999, these two subcommittees were put into operation for the first time and the "Memorandum of Agreement on Subcommittee Operating Procedures" was adopted

[434] And at the 5th meeting held in April of that year and the 6th meeting held in August, specific discussions on the agenda for the four-party talks were held through two subcommittees

[435] However, since the 6th round of talks broke down when North Korea insisted on the withdrawal of US troops from South Korea and the conclusion of a US-North Korea peace treaty as the substantive agenda of the four-party talks, no further talks have been held to this day

[436] However, our government, with the conviction that the four-party talks are the most useful method for establishing a peace regime on the Korean Peninsula, will continue to work to establish a peace regime on the Korean Peninsula in which South and North Korea take the lead and the United States and China participate and guarantee the talks

[437] And to further elevate

[438] The inter-Korean summit (June 2000) is taking place

[439] Accordingly, the government has continuously pursued policies to induce North Korea to become a place of reconciliation and cooperation, such as activating contact and dialogue between the South and the North, increasing economic exchange and cooperation, and activating humanitarian aid to the North. On March 9, 2000, through the so-called "Berlin Declaration," it declared four major practical tasks to both domestically and internationally: "supporting the recovery of the North Korean economy, pursuing an end to the Cold War and a peace settlement on the Korean Peninsula, resolving the issue of separated families, and holding dialogue between the authorities of the South and the North

[440] Thanks to our consistent policy of reconciliation and cooperation and the Berlin Declaration, North Korea gradually gained trust in our government's pro-North Korea policy, and finally, South and North Korea agreed to hold an inter-Korean summit

[441] Accordingly, a historic inter-Korean summit was held in Pyongyang from June 13th to 15th, and the leaders of the two Koreas agreed on the 'South-North Joint Declaration

[442] This marks the end of the conflict and confrontation between the South and the North that has persisted for over half a century, and marks a new starting point in the history of our nation for reconciliation, cooperation, and peace

[443] The reason the “June South-North Joint Declaration” could be adopted at the inter-Korean summit was, first, because of the historic decision of the leaders of the two Koreas to agree that dialogue and cooperation were the only ways to prevent the deepening of division, ensure common prosperity, and advance peaceful unification; second, because of the post-Cold War trend and the maturing atmosphere of peace on the Korean Peninsula, North Korea recognized the need to adjust its domestic and foreign policies and to improve relations with the South; third, because the South and the North sought productive dialogue to acknowledge their differences and find common ground based on mutual understanding and trust; fourth, because North Korea developed a positive perception of the government’s consistently pursued “policy of reconciliation and cooperation”; and fifth, because of the public support for the “policy of reconciliation and cooperation” and the cooperation of the international community

[444] The Inter-Korean Summit, the first meeting between the leaders of the two Koreas in 55 years since the division of the peninsula, not only implemented the principle of resolving the Korean Peninsula issue through the parties involved and formed a firm consensus on preventing the recurrence of war and establishing peace, but also served as an opportunity for normalizing inter-Korean relations and discussing unification issues based on reality

[445] In addition, this meeting served as a turning point for progress in inter-Korean relations, as it agreed to promote cooperation between the South and the North to resolve the issue of separated families and to promote friendly inter-Korean cooperation relations, and agreed to Chairman Kim Jong-il's return visit to Seoul, thereby guaranteeing the implementation of the agreement and exploring the possibility of regularizing inter-Korean summits

[446] Following the inter-Korean defense ministers' meeting and the inter-Korean summit, it was held to facilitate the reunion of separated families, and the first and second inter-Korean ministerial talks were held in succession to discuss putting inter-Korean exchanges and cooperation into practice, leading to active exchanges and cooperation between the two Koreas in the economic, social, and cultural fields

[447] However, in the military field, efforts to build trust and ease tensions between the military authorities of the South and the North have been relatively insufficient, and it has been pointed out that the smooth implementation of the 'June 15th South-North Joint Declaration' can only be achieved when the militaries of the South and the North can guarantee a definite peace in this region by freeing themselves from the distrust and confrontation of the past

[448] Accordingly, the government, judging that balanced progress in the areas of reconciliation, cooperation, and peace between the South and the North is important, continuously raised this issue with North Korea, and finally, at the 78th Inter-Korean Ministerial Talks held in Pyongyang on September 1, the South and the North reached an agreement to make efforts to guarantee peace and to discuss having the military authorities of both sides hold talks at an early date

[449] Kim Il-chol sent a letter to the Ministry of People's Armed Forces, revealing that it was a new one

[450] The meeting was held in Hong Kong on September 14th, and the military delegation that discussed and proposed the amendments was attended by a five-member delegation from the North Korean side, led by Minister of the People's Armed Forces Kim Il Chol, and the two sides formalized the results of the meeting in a joint press statement

[451] After the talks, the North Korean delegation paid a courtesy call on President Kim Dae-jung on the 3rd and then returned through Panmunjom

[452] The achievements and significance of the first inter-Korean defense ministers' meeting include the following

[453] First, it was the first time that the military authorities of the South and the North met in person and held talks since the division

[454] Considering that North Korea has insisted on negotiations with the United States on military issues, it is noteworthy that North Korea supports this meeting itself with the 'First South-North Defense Ministerial Talks; Jeju Island, September 25-26, 2000 Defense Ministerial Declaration', and by agreeing to actively cooperate with each other to resolve military issues arising from the meeting, they have created room for active dialogue and cooperation between the military authorities of the South and the North in the future

[455] With this, the implementation of the 'June 15th North-South Joint Declaration' has been more reliably guaranteed, providing an opportunity for the inter-Korean relationship, which had been centered on exchanges and cooperation in the economic, social, and cultural fields, to expand and develop into a military dimension

[456] Third, the defense ministers of both sides agreed that eliminating the risk of war through easing tensions between the South and the North and establishing a peace regime on the Korean Peninsula is an urgent matter, and they declared both domestically and internationally that they would make joint efforts to achieve this

[457] In the future, the military authorities of the South and the North will be able to seriously discuss ways to build military trust and implement measures to ease tensions, such as installing a direct telephone line between the military authorities of the South and the North to prevent accidental clashes, exchanging military personnel, observing and monitoring large-scale unit movements and training, exchanging military information, and peacefully utilizing the demilitarized zone, and ways to establish peace based on these measures

[458] Fourth, the restoration of the Gyeongui Line railway and the opening of the Gaeseong-Munsan road, which are pressing issues between the South and the North, require the prior resolution of related military issues. By agreeing in principle on security guarantees related to this, the railway and road connection construction project has begun in earnest

[459] Fifth, since the opening of a certain section of the Military Demarcation Line and the Demilitarized Zone is inevitable for the connection of railways and roads, the South and North Korean defense ministers agreed to handle the issue of establishing the South and North Korean jurisdictional areas based on the Armistice Agreement, thereby providing an opportunity to confirm the legal and practical validity of the Armistice Agreement

[460] This agreement reaffirms that the provisions of the Armistice Agreement must be observed until it is replaced by a peace treaty

[461] Sixth, by agreeing to hold the second round of talks in North Korea, the two sides have created the opportunity to make dialogue between the military authorities of the two Koreas virtually regular

[462] This opens the way for gradual consultation and exploration of military trust-building and tension-easing measures through future inter-Korean military authorities talks

[463] It is expected that future improvements in inter-Korean relations will be pursued in parallel centered on the axes of economic and social exchange and cooperation and another axis of easing military tensions and building trust

[464] If inter-Korean ministerial talks are the main channels for exchange and cooperation, then inter-Korean defense ministerial talks will be the main window for discussing issues of military trust-building and tension relief

[465] Everyone hopes that military tensions between the South and the North will ease and trust will be built in the future, but it is too early to expect this

[466] Dialogue between North and South Korean military authorities is just beginning

[467] Going forward, we must not neglect even the slightest effort to improve inter-Korean relations and maintain a firm security posture

[477] The government was made up of 'sacrificed soldiers' who fought for the country during the Korean War

[478] The UN Command had been demanding the repatriation of South Korean prisoners of war since the early 1960s, but North Korea followed suit and the issue of South Korean prisoners of war has not been resolved. Now, following the dramatic return of Second Lieutenant Cho Chang-ho in 1997, the UN is pushing for the repatriation of South Korean prisoners of war in the spirit of fulfilling the nation's duty and purpose

[479] In particular, considering that all of the prisoners of war are elderly, the government is seeking a prompt resolution from North Korea regarding the issue of prisoners of war, and plans to discuss this issue with North Korea in a broader sense as a solution to the issue of separated families. The government is seeking a reasonable solution to this issue by establishing the following punitive and institutional devices in relation to the issue of prisoners of war

[480] By processing the 24,000 missing persons who had been missing during the war, the bereaved families were able to receive compensation. By enacting and promulgating the 'Act on the Treatment of Prisoners of War, etc.', the national basis for support and treatment of prisoners of war and their families upon their return was established, and the stability of life and welfare of prisoners of war and their families were promoted

[481] In January 1999, the 'Government-wide Military POW Countermeasures Committee', chaired by the Vice Minister of National Defense, was established, thereby establishing an institutional mechanism to establish a cooperative system among relevant ministries

[482] We have set up a public complaint center in nine locations across the country to receive reports on missing persons from the Korean War, and we plan to actively promote this by reflecting it in the policy on prisoners of war presumed to be alive

[483] At the government level, we are continuously reporting countermeasures to expand domestic connections. --- Page 83 --- 1. Arms control on the Korean Peninsula [| Arms control is a military strategy that cannot be achieved through consultation on national security objectives

[484] Therefore, arms control focuses on eliminating the military threat motivation of an enemy country in advance through trust-building and improving the security environment, achieving a balance of military power at a lower level, and limiting mutual attack capabilities. In order to reduce military tensions on the Korean Peninsula, where a sharp military standoff persists, and to implement a stable military relationship, our government is advancing arms control from the perspective of national security

[485] Therefore, if North Korea responds to control measures, including military trust-building, arms restrictions, and arms reduction, we will actively negotiate and pursue them

[486] In order to achieve the goal, the two Koreas have been actively seeking to control arms between the South and the North by actively responding to changes in domestic and international situations and increasing national security

[487] The government is pursuing an arms control policy with the goal of establishing peaceful coexistence through easing tensions between the South and the North and, furthermore, laying the foundation for peaceful unification

[488] In other words, the U.S. government's arms control policy is being promoted with a focus on feasibility and the common interests and prosperity of the nation

[489] Our arms control policy is basically a gradual and step-by-step approach based on the principle of “confidence building first, disarmament later.”

[490] At the political level, it is to recognize and promote mutual systems and to promote exchanges and cooperation in each field, including non-aggression and non-internal affairs. At the military level, it is to secure military stability by exchanging information on mutual military activities, enhancing military transparency and predictability, and limiting and eliminating striking capabilities. Our arms control policy aims to eliminate the risk of war on the Korean Peninsula, establish a Korean Peninsula common ground, and lay the foundation for unification. We are maintaining the following military control policy keynote

[491] First, inter-Korean arms control should be promoted through inter-Korean dialogue under the principle of direct resolution of Korean Peninsula issues between the parties involved

[492] Meanwhile, North Korea has been trying to resolve the Korean Peninsula issue through direct dialogue with the United States while neutralizing the armistice system

[493] However, the government maintains a firm stance that issues related to peace and stability on the Korean Peninsula must be resolved directly by the two Koreas

[494] Accordingly, the government is striving to promote arms control through direct dialogue and bilateral negotiations between North and South Korea under close cooperation between South Korea and the United States

[495] In addition, we are actively pursuing discussions with countries involved in the Korean Peninsula issue that have not been able to proceed through the four-party talks

[496] Second, it is to give priority to the implementation of matters already agreed upon in the agreement

[497] There has already been an agreement between North and South Korea on a basic agreement system as an intermediate step toward transitioning from an armistice system to a peace system, but North Korea is ignoring it

[498] The Basic Agreement includes arms control measures that are desperately needed on the Korean Peninsula, such as military confidence-building measures and the control of weapons of mass destruction. Prioritizing the implementation of these agreements is the most efficient and desirable way to promote inter-Korean arms control

[499] It is desirable to discuss and promote detailed implementation matters based on the “Supplementary Agreement on the Implementation and Compliance of Non-Aggression between the South and the North” of the South-North Basic Agreement through the South-North Joint Military Committee

[500] The government is developing measures to encourage North Korea to return to the inter-Korean basic agreement system

[501] Third, taking into account the progress in inter-Korean relations and changes in North Korea's attitude, we will responsively pursue a combination of trust-building, arms restrictions, and arms reduction depending on the situation

[502] It is assessed that the historic inter-Korean summit and subsequent inter-Korean ministerial talks have significantly created conditions for promoting arms control on the Korean Peninsula

[503] Taking into account the differences in positions between North and South Korea on the promotion of arms control, the government plans to flexibly propose and propose phased arms control measures in line with the progress of easing tensions and building trust

[504] That is, in addition to basic trust-building measures such as the installation of a direct telephone line between the military authorities of the South and the North to prevent accidental armed conflicts as specified in the Basic Agreement, exchange of military personnel, joint reporting and observation of large-scale unit movements and military exercises, exchange of military governments, and peaceful use of the demilitarized zone, the policy is to pursue measures to halt the arms race through a freeze on weapons and pilot disarmament projects in parallel

[505] North Korea's development programs for weapons of mass destruction, including nuclear weapons, chemical and biological weapons, and missiles, are recognized as serious security threats that could undermine stability and peace not only on the Korean Peninsula but also in Northeast Asia

[506] In order to resolve the threat of North Korea's weapons of mass destruction, not only should arms control negotiations be held between the South and the North, but also close cooperation and support from the international community are required, in addition to the suspension of construction on the North Korean light-water reactor project site

[507] In the "Agreed Framework" signed between the United States and North Korea in October 1994, in exchange for a comprehensive resolution of the North Korean nuclear issue, including freezing North Korea's nuclear development, dismantling related nuclear facilities, and sealing and transferring spent fuel rods overseas, it was agreed that two 11,000-megawatt light-water reactors would be provided, 500,000 tons of heavy fuel oil would be provided annually until the first light-water reactor was completed, inter-Korean dialogue would be resumed, and economic sanctions would be eased

[508] Accordingly, the Korean Peninsula Energy Development Organization (KEPCO) was established with participation from Korea, the United States, Japan, and others, and the North Korea Light Water Reactor Supply Agreement was signed on December

[509] Since the groundbreaking ceremony for the light-water reactor project in Sinpo, North Korea in August 1997, the project has been progressing smoothly

[510] Negotiations have been concluded among South Korea, the United States, and Japan regarding the share of the total project cost of $4.6 billion, and heavy fuel oil, which is being provided as alternative fuel until the construction of the first light-water reactor is completed, will continue to be delivered to the North

[511] The sealing work on approximately 8,000 spent fuel rods from North Korea's Yongbyon 5-megawatt nuclear reactor, in accordance with the Geneva Agreed Framework, was also completed in April

[512] South Korea, which is responsible for 70% of the cost of constructing light-water reactors, will continue to faithfully play a central role in providing support for the reactors, assuming that North Korea faithfully implements the terms of the agreement

[513] Accordingly, North Korea must not only freeze its nuclear development as stipulated in the US-North Korea Geneva Agreed Framework, but also faithfully fulfill its obligations to the International Atomic Energy Agency for verification and inspection to ensure transparency on past nuclear issues that the entire world is concerned about. In addition, North Korea must faithfully implement the “Joint Declaration on the Denuclearization of the Korean Peninsula,” which came into effect on January 20, 1992, in line with progress in inter-Korean dialogue

[514] The Republic of Korea should also join the Chemical Weapons Convention (2000) and the Missile Technology Control Regime and will to participate in international efforts for peace and stability

[515] In this regard, if North Korea transparently demonstrates its own efforts, it will not only solidify the current improvement in inter-Korean relations, but also create conditions for further improvement in relations with advanced Western countries through the lifting of international economic sanctions, and for increased economic cooperation such as joining international economic organizations. Furthermore, it will be able to enhance its own status as a member of the UN Security Council. Through the last inter-Korean summit, we also discussed this issue. It has been a month since the North Korean leader Kim Jong-un's provocation, but the government's firm judgment is that North Korea can contribute to peace on the Korean Peninsula and in the world by accepting the international control system related to weapons of mass destruction

[516] Going forward, we will continue to promote inter-Korean dialogue for reconciliation, cooperation, and trust-building, while North Korea actively participates in the international non-proliferation system and develops and operates weapons of mass destruction

[517] Page 87 --- Chapter 4 Foreign Military Policy to Contribute to the Recognition of the Korean Peninsula and World Peace Section 1 The ROK-US Alliance and Our Security By establishing a practical 8-way system to establish a system of shareholders rather than a new one - The two countries, which have been green-to-green for the past half-century, have established a secret ROK-US Defense Ministerial Meeting (March 2000) ㆍ The security partnership between the two countries will guarantee peace by deterring war on the Korean Peninsula until peaceful unification of North and South Korea is achieved, and will continue to serve as a ram for the security of the Northeast Asian region even after unification

[518] •The United States made a treaty on peace, trade, and navigation, and in 1888, the United States formed a four-man interpreting officer liaison office, marking the beginning of military relations between the two countries

[519] However, the modern era began in earnest with the arrival of the US military in order to disarm the defeated Japanese army, and the Korean War that broke out soon after became an important opportunity for the security and foreign policy of both Korea and the US

[520] The ROK-US Mutual Defense Treaty was signed in Washington on October 1, 1953, immediately after the armistice of the Korean War, and came into effect on November 18, 1954, the following year

[521] The Mutual Defense Treaty between the Republic of Korea and the United States is a true treaty established by the two countries to jointly respond to any invasion of Korea by external forces. It greatly contributes not only to the security of Korea but also to the stability of the entire Northeast Asian region. The Mutual Defense Treaty between the Republic of Korea and the United States is the legal basis for the ROK-US combined defense system, and provides the basis for various security and military-related matters between governments or military authorities, including the Status of Forces Agreement

[522] Development of ROK-US security cooperation and joint defense system

[523] The ROK-US Security Consultative Meeting has been held annually since the January 21 Incident and the Pueblo kidnapping incident in 1968 to discuss and resolve major security issues

[524] Starting with the first meeting in Washington in May 1968, a total of 39 meetings were held until 2000. From the fourth meeting in 1971, the name was changed to the "ROK-US Security Consultative Meeting" and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs also participated, developing it into a meeting of true security dimensions to this day. This meeting consists of a plenary session for the Defense Ministers' Meeting and one meeting of five working-level subcommittees for security

[525] The 32nd Separate Meeting of the 5014 Joint Chiefs of Staff (September 2000): ㆍ Under the authority of the 'National Command and Military Command Organizations of the two countries, 040144)', the Military Committee 040) issues strategic instructions, and the Military Committee, chaired by the Chairman of the Joint Chiefs of Staff of the two countries, is the superior organization of the ROK-US Combined Forces Command, issues strategic instructions and operational instructions to the ROK-US Combined Forces Command and assigns missions

[526] The plenary session of the 'Military Committee' is held before the 'ROK-US Security Consultative Meeting' and its results are reported to the 'Security Consultative Meeting

[527] Military | Standing meetings are held as needed, and the 'ROK-US Security Consultative Meeting' related to the US Combined Forces Command has been given importance for its symbolic significance as a regular meeting between the ministers of the two countries, but since the late 1980s, it has been a practical policy consultation body that discusses major security issues between ROK and the US, prepares countermeasures, and jointly designs the long-term development direction of the military relationship between the two countries. The actual operating entity of the ROK-US combined defense system is the ROK-US Combined Forces Command, which was established in November of the same year based on the "Military Committee and ROK-US Combined Forces Command Delegation of Authority (76280) agreed upon at the 10th 8044 on the 36th of the month and the Strategic Directive No. 1 issued at the Military Committee Meeting in

[528] The ROK-U.S. joint defense system is fundamentally a matter of mutual benefit between the two countries

[529] By integrating forces and operating them, the operational control over designated ROK military units on the Korean Peninsula, which had been exercised by the UN Commander since the Korean War, was delegated to the ROK-US Combined Forces Commander

[530] However, as of December 1, 1994, the peacetime operational control authority that the ROK-US Combined Forces Commander had exercised over ROK military units was transferred to the Chairman of the ROK Joint Chiefs of Staff, and thus, in peacetime, the ROK-US Combined Forces Commander was only responsible for matters delegated by the Chairman of the ROK Joint Chiefs of Staff

[531] However, the maintenance of the armistice agreement remains the responsibility of the Commander-in-Chief of the United Nations Command, and in this regard, the United Nations Command and the Combined Forces Command are in a relationship of mutual support and cooperation

[532] Since 1991, our government has comprehensively considered the level of defense cost sharing of the stationed U.S. troops by allied countries, the contribution of the U.S. forces in Korea to the defense of Korea, and the need to develop the ROK-U.S. military alliance into a “partnership of security cooperation,” and has shared a portion of the cost of stationing U.S. forces in Korea. In the first defense cost agreement (1991 Special 006) concluded in 1991, it was stipulated that the U.S. would share one-third of the total cost of stationing U.S. forces in Korea, excluding U.S. personnel costs

[533] Subsequently, Korea and the United States signed the Special Agreement on Defense Cost Sharing for the three years from 1996 to 1998, and agreed to increase the cost sharing by 10% each year compared to the previous year, based on the 1995 support amount of $300 million

[534] Accordingly, our defense budget per unit in 1996 reached 399 million dollars, but it was adjusted to 310 million dollars in late 1997, taking into account Korea's economic conditions due to the worsening foreign exchange situation

[535] Subsequently, South Korea and the United States signed an agreement on defense cost sharing for 1999 and beyond on February

[536] According to the agreement, the defense cost sharing for 1999 was KRW 257.5 billion and $141.9 million, which is approximately $333 million when converted to

[537] In addition, the defense cost sharing for 2000 and 2001 was linked to the nominal GDP growth rate of the previous year, so that defense cost sharing support was provided based on our burden capacity

[538] Accordingly, the defense cost sharing for 2000 is approximately $391 million, a 9.78% increase from the 1999 agreement, and the defense cost sharing for 2001 is under negotiation

[539] The resolution of all pending issues between Korea and the United States can be promoted through this network to the people around the country

[540] As a member of the ROK–US combined defense system, the US Forces Korea plays an important role in the defense of Korea. However, public interest has been growing recently regarding various issues arising from the operation and training of US bases in Korea

[541] However, this is a problem that arises at the level of management of the ROK-US alliance, so the principle for resolving the problem should be to present a win-win plan for the national interest of both the ROK-US alliance and the people

[542] In accordance with this recognition, the governments of Korea and the United States are working closely with the relevant authorities of both countries to devise a plan to resolve some of the problems caused by the US military stationed in Korea through various means. Regarding the issue of revising the SOFA, the government is closely consulting with the relevant authorities of both countries to devise a plan to resolve the impact on the environment of revising some provisions related to criminal jurisdiction that is disadvantageous compared to other countries

[543] In addition, in order to alleviate the inconvenience to residents around the Maehyang-ri US Air Force bombing range, drastic measures have been implemented to reduce noise and improve safety, such as suspending machine gun firing training and restricting the disposal of ammunition since August

[544] Regarding other issues related to the US Forces Korea, both Korea and the US are ignoring the 806 Joint Committee and other bilateral measures

[545] The two countries of Korea and the United States will continue to develop the security cooperation relationship that has made crucial contributions to both sides over the past 50 years and continue efforts to minimize barriers

[546] Accordingly, the purpose of the establishment of the base and the training of the combined forces is to deter war on the Korean Peninsula and to build mutual trust through military exchanges and cooperation with friendly countries, thereby increasing peace and stability not only on the Korean Peninsula but also in the region

[547] In particular, developing military relations with Japan, China, and Russia in a balanced manner is crucial for promoting international understanding of the Korean Peninsula, and further contributes to regional stability and international peace

[548] These military diplomatic activities with neighboring countries have been developing more broadly since the 1990s

[549] The 'People's Government' has successfully concluded summits with neighboring countries to secure their understanding in exchange for the policy of reconciliation and cooperation, and thus, we have military diplomacy toward our neighboring countries
