[1116] Meanwhile, the Ministry of National Defense is actively participating in international military environmental cooperation to acquire advanced environmental technologies and information from advanced countries' militaries and to strengthen the status of our military. In addition, it is studying and reviewing the "Development Plan for an International Military Environmental Cooperation System" to establish a foundation for comprehensive military environmental cooperation

[1117] The development of environmental research activities is a continuous research project to eliminate the unique environmental hazards of the military and to establish an ‘environmental management system establishment plan.’

[1118] Through the “Military Environmental Conservation Academic Conference" held periodically since 1998, the military environmental experts and environmental experts have been motivated to conduct environmental research

[1119] We are encouraging and promoting environmental conservation awareness among soldiers. 2006 Go & * Military Environmental Conservation Academic Conference Garrison Environmental Pollution Prevention Project A

[1120] Expansion of environmental infrastructure and equipment The military has many places where its bases and main activity areas are closely related to water source protection areas, but military bases are difficult to access due to special laws such as the Military Installations Protection Act. Therefore, the military, which has the authority and responsibility for environmental conservation in its jurisdiction, is in dire need of its own efforts to conserve the environment

[1121] From this perspective, the military is quickly securing legal treatment facilities and equipment, such as sewage treatment facilities, to prevent violations of environmental laws and regulations in the military and to fundamentally prevent environmental pollution caused by military stationing and operations

[1122] The military plans to expand sewage treatment facilities to 706 locations by the end of 2000, and to complete sewage treatment facilities in the Han and Nakdong River basins by 2001, in the Geum and Yeongsan River basins by 2002, and in all other regions by

[1123] Figure 5-9] Status of possession and acquisition plan of environmental facilities/equipment Category 'Branch | Total required ~ 2000 km: 12001~2005 Corruption No. Total (%) | Cumulative (%) | Environmental basic facilities (places) 4,427 764 37 87 19 3.576 | 100 Environmental equipment (points) | 5789 | 3,400 | 5 100 Gaz

[1124] ※ Environmental facilities include 2,256 soil pollution prevention facilities (51% of total facilities

[1125] In addition, a total of 4,437 environmental facilities, including incinerators and soil pollution prevention facilities, will be expanded by 2005, and renovation and maintenance work will be carried out on existing environmental infrastructure with insufficient functions

[1126] Meanwhile, in order to keep pace with the development trends of environmental facilities and equipment, we are actively carrying out environmental promotion activities such as collecting new information and holding exhibitions, and we are also in the practical stage of developing reed wetlands, water hyacinths, and water parsley beds

[1127] The Waste Management and Resource Recycling Division is working to prevent environmental pollution and recycle waste through strict waste management

[1128] Military-generated waste is either incinerated in the military's own incinerator or disposed of by local governments or disposal companies through military vehicles or volume-based dumping systems

[1129] Meanwhile, designated wastes that can aggravate environmental pollution, such as waste oil, waste acid, and waste alkali, are handled by specialized waste disposal companies other than those handled in-house for recycling, thereby reducing environmental pollution

[1130] As of the end of 1999, 19,991 waste separation collection stations were installed and operated throughout the county to reduce waste and recycle resources

[1131] Figure 5-10] Status of military waste generation and disposal (1999, 42 (units) Category Generation amount

[1132] Consignment processing | Self-disposal Total 222,424 151,726 44,433 12,114 14151 General household waste 147,053 118,745 15838 11.601 869 Waste | Food waste 32780 6,992 25331 457 - Designated waste 19,130 9,118 2386 0 7,626 Construction waste

[1133] When promoting various military facility construction project plans, the military must comply with all procedures under the Environmental Impact Assessment Act and address any complaints from local residents that may arise due to military operations

[1134] In order to efficiently carry out human resources management duties such as devising and operating environmental facilities consignment management plans, it is realistically difficult to secure environmental facilities within a short period of time for each unit

[1135] Accordingly, taking this reality into account, the military is currently reviewing a plan to develop and expand the environmental management system currently being implemented in some units in order to secure the professionalism, economy, and efficiency of military environmental management while minimizing the loss of combat power

[1136] Utilizing the expertise of private environmental management companies to manage military environmental facilities will not only solve environmental problems that are currently a significant burden on commanders of all levels of units, but will also greatly contribute to strengthening public trust in military environmental management

[1137] Strengthening natural environment conservation activities

[1138] The military is actively participating in local environmental cleanup activities around the bases in parallel with environmental pollution prevention activities within the barracks, thereby strengthening its efforts to clean up the people's living spaces and preserve the natural ecosystem

[1139] In particular, the Navy is carrying out marine environment improvement projects, such as forming and operating island volunteer groups in approximately 60 islands scattered across the West and South Seas with the cooperation of local governments and the Ministry of Oceans and Fisheries, and the Army is promoting the Baekdudaegan environmental restoration project in a public-private partnership

[1140] The military's jurisdictional area for promoting natural environment conservation measures in garrison and operationaltraining areas is not only vast in area, but also located in many areas important for natural ecosystem conservation, such as forest areas, natural ecosystem conservation areas, and clean areas

[1141] Military activities such as operations and training are often carried out directly in the natural environment, requiring special attention and effort from the military to preserve the natural environment in the region

[1142] The military's scope of activity will expand across the entire target area, and artificial changes will be made to the natural environment, such as installing trenches, obstacles, and lodging facilities everywhere and removing trees to secure visibility

[1143] This situation is repeated every year during various training sessions at all levels of the military, and the military is raising the environmental conservation awareness of commanders and soldiers and making sure to consider measures for environmental conservation when establishing training plans, minimizing damage to the natural environment during training and restoring any natural environment that is inevitably damaged after training to its original state as much as possible

[1144] Soil contamination caused by oil leaks from oil storage facilities in the process of promoting contaminated soil restoration projects not only pollutes the soil but also causes problems such as groundwater contamination and devastation of surrounding crops, and also becomes a cause of public complaints

[1145] The military has completed the first soil contamination test for approximately 2,000 oil storage facilities at approximately 1300 locations, and plans to complete restoration of contaminated soil by 2010 according to the annual plan in order of priority

[1146] Figure 5-11] Current status of soil contamination inspection/plan for oil storage facilities Category Total 1998 Implementation 1999 Implementation 2000 Plan Soil contamination (times) 1,242 228 623 391 ※ Facilities subject to inspection: Oil storage facilities with a capacity of 20,000 liters or more (restoration sites are estimated to be 25% of target facilities). Among the contaminated soil restoration projects at the old maintenance depot sites in Busan that have been in progress since 1997, the restoration of the first maintenance depot area has been completed, and the soil contamination survey for the third maintenance depot area has been completed and a tree garden project is being promoted in cooperation with related organizations such as Busan City

[1147] The military is actively pursuing projects to restore contaminated soil caused by oil spills, landfills, etc., but soil restoration technologies for various types of contamination have not yet been sufficiently developed

[1148] In addition, there are unique contamination-causing factors that arise from the operation of specific weapon systems, and it is urgent to develop independent technologies and establish restoration procedures for contaminated environments

[1149] Accordingly, the military is promoting research projects to develop technologies for restoring polluted environments. These studies are sometimes conducted with the military's own budget, and sometimes by external research institutes that recognize the necessity and importance of the research

[1150] In relation to this, the “pilot project for restoration of contaminated soil” due to oil contamination was implemented in the Navy’s jurisdiction area under the cooperation of the Korea Institute of Science and Technology (1998), and in 1999, a research project was promoted to establish procedures and systems for restoration of contaminated soil

[1151] Meanwhile, when promoting decontamination treatment and restoration projects for polluted areas, we plan to objectively set pollution tolerance standards and treatment goals for each stage and link related information for each stage until restoration is completed to secure efficiency and transparency in project promotion, and to establish a system that can utilize accumulated purified water^ Nasi} business

[1152] The Ministry of National Defense has established the 'Military Work Environment and Worker Mortgage Management Regulations' and the 'Military Radiation Safety Rights Regulations' as directives, and has established specific measures to manage hazardous environmental factors within military facilities, such as heavy metals, organic solvents, noise, dust, and medical and industrial radiation, and to protect the health of military workers

[1153] In 1999, a military radiation safety inspection agency was established within the Army General Maintenance Depot to manage safety, and special health examination agencies and work environment measurement agencies are planned to be established in each military branch by 2002 to manage the health of military workers

[1154] The military is developing measures to prevent health risks to soldiers and environmental pollution caused by hazardous chemicals, and plans to implement the "Guidelines for the Safety Management of Hazardous Chemicals for Military Use" in early

[1155] In addition, by creating a database of management methods for each substance and posting it on the Defense Intranet, information on hazardous substances can be searched anywhere within the military, and we plan to continuously update the data for additional hazardous substances in the future

[1156] Local Communities and Military Facilities: Consolidation of Private Land Used by the Military Our military has been steadily pursuing compensation and consolidation of private land occupied and used by the military to protect the property rights of the people

[1157] To this end, private land that needs to be continuously used by the military is being purchased and compensated for in accordance with the “Special Act on Acquisition of Public Land and Compensation for Loss,” and private land that does not need to be continuously used is being returned to the owner immediately

[1158] In addition, if the owner of private land used by the military wishes to exchange it for idle national land, we actively accept this, and since 1995, we have been actively purchasing and compensating for private land used by the military not only with the general budget but also with the special account budget for national property management using the proceeds from the sale of idle national land

[1159] In addition, even in cases where there is an urgent need for military purposes, the landowner's consent for use must be obtained in advance or the land must be purchased through legal procedures. The military's performance in organizing private land so far is as follows

[1162] As shown in the chart, a total of 3.1 trillion won was invested from 1966 to 1999 to relocate 209 units, and in 2000, a project to relocate 44 units was promoted with a budget of 200.1 billion won, and 12 of them were relocated

[1164] We plan to continuously pursue the military facility relocation project by donating to the military and taking over existing military facilities in return

[1165] Improvement of the system and deregulation related to military facility protection zones In order to protect important military facilities and smoothly carry out military operations, laws related to the protection of military facilities (Military Facilities Protection Act, Naval Base Act, Military Air Base Act) were enacted and 3.3 billion won was allocated to the contact area and the surroundings of major military facilities based on the law

[1166] We have been managing an area of approximately ten million pyeong as a military facility (base) protection zone

[1167] However, as the use of military facilities in many parts of the country is restricted and causes inconvenience to the lives of residents, we are continuously reviewing the improvement of the system related to military facility protection zones and areas where the restrictions can be relaxed to promote public convenience within the scope of guaranteeing military operations

[1168] First, various laws and regulations related to military facility protection zones were enacted and revised from 1997 to the first half of 1999, and some of the village areas within the civilian control line northern control protection zone, unification policy-related business areas, and security tourism facility areas were changed to restricted protection zones

[1169] Until recently, no new or expanded housing was permitted within the ammunition depot protection zone, but in cases where houses within the protection zone must be demolished due to public projects such as railway or road construction, the law was revised to allow relocation

[1170] In addition, the protection zone of the support aircraft base, which was 50 meters from the outer boundary of the airfield, was reduced to 2 meters, and the helicopter-only operation base was also reduced and adjusted to ease the regulatory scope around the airbase

[1171] Second, starting in August 1998, the "preliminary analysis system" for military facility protection zones was implemented, simplifying administrative procedures by posting a "preliminary analysis map" specifying the allowable heights of various buildings within military facility protection zones to administrative agencies and allowing residents to view and utilize it

[1172] In November 1999, the task of negotiating military protection zones covering approximately 1,500 pyeong in 34 regions, including Paju, Gyeonggi-do, and Yeoncheon, was delegated to administrative agencies

[1173] Third, in June 1998, 3,272,000 pyeong of land in 10 areas, including the metropolitan development area, that had no impact on military operations were released from the protection zone

[1174] And, 2.33 million pyeong of 16 village areas were changed from controlled protection zones to restricted protection zones, thereby easing the restrictions on building expansion and renovation by local residents

[1176] Military support for public welfare and national projects

[1177] Military's Civil Support Project Our military actively supports the public by utilizing its personnel, equipment, and technology in areas that do not require military assistance, without being restricted from performing its basic missions. In particular, we are supporting regular rice planting and harvesting in vulnerable areas of the agricultural sector, which is having difficulties in farming in a timely manner due to labor shortages in rural areas

[1178] In addition, we are expanding agricultural infrastructure such as irrigation canal maintenance and farmland management, and we are also providing technical support such as organizing an agricultural machinery repair service team to visit areas where agricultural machinery service centers are not established and repair agricultural machinery

[1179] Figure 5-15] Status of rural labor assistance in 1999 772,931 people 5,593 units 5,614 information 7,659 units 21,707 179 tons 4, 694 something Meanwhile, the military is providing free medical treatment and quarantine activities to medically vulnerable areas and is promoting a 'movement to form sisterhood ties between one or more units and social welfare facilities or underprivileged neighbors in the vicinity

[1180] We also carry out ongoing volunteer activities throughout the year, including providing free legal consultations to help local residents with their legal difficulties

[1181] When foot-and-mouth disease broke out in Paju and Hongseong, Gyeonggi Province, over a four-month period starting in late August 2000, an average of 330 military personnel and 90 quarantine vehicles were provided daily to prevent the spread of the infectious disease

[1182] The National Project Support Group has continued to participate in national land development projects, such as road construction and rural land consolidation projects, to increase public convenience and develop national key industries

[1183] From 1990 to 1993, we provided troops and equipment for the Jayuro roadbed construction project, and in 1996, we completed the Baengnyeongdo and Daecheongdo road improvement project

[1184] Recently, from May 1997 to September 1999, troops and equipment were deployed to the construction of the Ganghwado Coastal Ring Road, completing the roadbed construction of section 1910-2. As a second-stage construction project, from June 2000, various earthworks such as heavy construction companies and excavators have been supported for section 9.519-2, and this project is scheduled to continue until December

[1185] In addition, it is expected that the support of the same military will save about 18 billion won in the national budget, and when this construction is completed, not only will the long-awaited projects of local residents in the border area, such as transportation, tourism, and farming, be resolved, but it will also become a <big> agricultural power < [2nd episode 095-un-pa-tu 00 --- Page 193 --- Disaster and catastrophe support activities a

[1186] Our military's disaster and disaster support activities are carried out in accordance with the 'Disaster Response Work Processing Regulations' and the 'Search and Rescue Headquarters Organization and Operation Rules' based on the 'Natural Disaster Countermeasures Act' and the 'Disaster Management Act' at the national level, and we have been making various efforts such as establishing a multifaceted readiness posture for active rescue support activities. First, we have established and are operating a search and rescue unit. The search and rescue unit is comprised of 18 units with special capabilities under the Maritime Operations Command and the Air Force Operations Command. The Forces Headquarters has been established and is always on standby, and depending on the type of aircraft accident or ship incident, we dispatch a unit appropriate for the mission

[1187] Third, a disaster relief unit is being established and operated

[1188] The military has designated one rescue unit per city and province to provide emergency recovery support upon request from administrative offices or reports from residents

[1189] Disaster relief units and administrative offices have a zero-level cooperation system and periodically conduct joint training and demonstrations of civilian-government-military disaster relief efforts

[1190] Third, the command system was unified to provide effective structural support

[1191] That is, ground support was placed under the responsibility of the Army Corps Commander to enable integrated command of the Army, Navy, and Air Force's reinforcement forces and equipment, and maritime support was placed under the responsibility of the Fleet Commander

[1192] Fourth, in case a large-scale natural disaster is expected, a disaster response situation room has been set up in units above the regiment level, and recovery equipment such as snowplows, bulldozers, and forklifts have been pre-deployed in the vicinity of heavy snow and heavy rain to prepare for it. Fifth, when support is provided anywhere in the country, a phone number (1339) has been set up that automatically connects to the relevant area, and a 'support first, report later' system has been established where the troops and equipment of the unit closest to the disaster area are available for the convenience of residents reporting

[1193] Sixth, in order to improve disaster relief support capabilities, a budget of 3.7 billion won was allocated between 1996 and 1999 to secure life-saving equipment, and 870 million won worth of equipment is scheduled to be secured by the end of

[1194] The military plans to reflect the purchase of life-saving equipment in its mid-term plan and to improve the search and rescue capabilities of the equipment

[1195] Seventh, in the event of a disaster, we are providing support to the public based on a "comprehensive concept" of medical care, quarantine, laundry, and water restrictions

[1196] In particular, in order to ensure the mobility of emergency patients and critically ill patients in areas where ambulance access is difficult, we operate an air medical evacuation squadron

[1197] Page 194 --- 794 I am all in this together

[1198] Disaster and Disaster Relief Activities Performance As shown in natural disasters in 1999 were different from those in the past in that they occurred in localized and simultaneous manner, and their scale also tended to become larger

[1199] Accordingly, the military carried out rapid recovery support activities by deploying as much available troops and equipment as possible, excluding essential operational troops

[1200] During the heavy rains in August 1999, military units also suffered many casualties

[1201] Although the damage was inflicted on the military, under the policy of “supporting the people first, then restoring the military damage,” support was provided to the people first, and then the military damage was restored

[1202] Going forward, the military, as the people's army, will actively support in the event of various disasters and calamities, and will enhance the nation's ability to manage its military equipment by strengthening substantial training and equipment to minimize damage to the lives and property of the people

(In accordance with the will of the entire nation that desires the peaceful reunification of the fatherland that came into effect on February 19, 1992, we reaffirm the stipulations made in the July 4th South-North Joint Statement, and we pledge to resolve the political and military confrontation to achieve national reconciliation, prevent the use of force and ensure the easing of tensions and peace, and realize multifaceted exchanges and cooperation to achieve the common interests of the people and the relationship between the two sides, recognizing that it is a provisional relationship in the process of pursuing the same, not a relationship between countries, and to make joint efforts to achieve peaceful reunification

[1387] Fat Country ㅇㅇ Chapter 1 South-North Reconciliation Article 2 The South and the North shall not interfere in each other's internal affairs

[1388] Article 3 The South and the North shall not slander or defame each other

[1389] Article 4 The South and the North shall not engage in any acts aimed at destroying or overthrowing the other

[1390] Article 5 The South and the North shall jointly endeavor to transform the present state of armistice into a solid state of peace between the South and the North, and shall abide by the present Military Armistice Agreement until such a state of peace is achieved

[1391] Article 6 of the Korean Declaration of Independence: The South and the North shall cease confrontation and competition on the international stage, cooperate with each other, and work together for the dignity and interests of the people

[1392] Article 7: To ensure close contact and cooperation between the South and the North, the South and the North shall establish and operate a South-North Liaison Office at Panmunjom within three months of the entry into force of this Agreement

[1393] Article 8: Within one month of the promulgation of this agreement, the South and the North shall establish a South-North political subcommittee within the framework of the main talks to discuss specific measures for the implementation and observance of the purpose of South-North reconciliation

[1394] Chapter 2 Non-aggression of the North and the South Article 9 The South and the North shall not use force against each other and shall not plunder each other by force

[1395] Article 10. The South and the North shall peacefully resolve differences of opinion and disputes through dialogue and negotiation

[1396] The non-aggression border and zones between the South and the North shall be the Military Demarcation Line stipulated in the Military Armistice Agreement of July 27, 1953 and the zones that each side has had jurisdiction over until now

[1397] Article 19. In order to ensure the equal protection of the two Koreas, the South and the North shall establish and operate a joint committee for the promotion of the two Koreas within eight months of the entry into force of this agreement

[1398] The South-North Joint Military Committee shall discuss issues related to the creation of military confidence and disarmament, including large-scale movement of units and the coordination and control of military exercises, peaceful use of the Demilitarized Zone, exchange of military personnel and information, phased disarmament including the elimination of weapons of mass destruction and attack capabilities, and verification. Article 18 The South and the North shall establish and operate a direct telephone line between the military authorities of both sides to prevent accidental armed conflicts and their escalation

[1399] Article 14 Within one month of the entry into force of this agreement, the two sides will discuss the implementation and compliance of the agreement on the inter-Korean military subcommittee on aggression and the establishment of a meeting to discuss remedial measures to resolve military confrontation. In order to promote equal and harmonious development and the well-being of the entire nation, economic exchanges and cooperation, such as exchanges of goods and joint investments, will be carried out as exchanges of resources

[1400] ㆍ Exchanges and cooperation are carried out in various fields such as technology, education, literature, arts, health, sports, environment, newspapers, radio, television, and reporting

[1401] Article 17 The South and the North shall realize free movement and contact between their members

[1402] Article 18 The South and the North shall implement free correspondence, exchanges of letters, meetings and visits between separated family members and relatives, realize the reunification of the two Koreas in freedom, and seek solutions to other humanitarian issues

[1403] Article 19 The South and the North shall reconnect severed railways and roads and open sea and air routes

[1404] Article 20 The South and the North shall install and connect facilities necessary for postal and telecommunication exchanges, and guarantee the confidentiality of postal and telecommunication exchanges

[1405] Article 21: The South and the North shall cooperate with each other in various fields, including economy and culture, on the international stage and jointly advance into foreign affairs

[1406] In order to implement the agreement to realize exchanges and cooperation in various fields, including economy and culture, the South and the North shall establish and operate a sector-specific joint committee, supplemented by the South-North Joint Committee on Economic Exchange and Cooperation, within three months after the entry into force of this Agreement. Within one month after the entry into force of this Agreement, the South and the North shall establish a South-North Exchange and Cooperation Subcommittee within the framework of the main talks to discuss specific measures for the implementation and compliance with the agreement on South-North Exchange and Cooperation

[1407] Amendment and Entry into Force Article 34 This Agreement may be amended or supplemented by mutual agreement

[1408] Article 26 This agreement shall come into effect on the date when the South and the North exchange their texts after each has completed the necessary procedures for its entry into force

[1410] Chapter 1 Recognition and Respect for the System Article 1 The South and the North shall recognize and respect each other's political, economic, social, and cultural systems

[1412] Article 3 The South and the North shall recognize and respect the authority and capabilities of each other's authorities

[1417] Chapter 3 Cessation of Defamation and Libel Article 8 The South and the North shall not defame or slander each other through the press, leaflets, or any other means or methods

[1418] Article 9 The South and the North shall not launch targeted attacks on specific individuals of the other side

[1419] Do not slander or defame the authorities of the other party

[1420] Article 18 The South and the North shall not slander or defame each other through broadcasts, visual media (posts), or any other means the Military Demarcation Line area. The South and the North shall not slander or defame each other at mass gatherings and events

[1421] Chapter 4 Prohibition of Acts of Destruction and Subversion Article 15 The South and the North shall not commit any acts of destruction or subversion against each other, whether direct or indirect, by violent or non-violent means, including terrorism, co-optation, homicide, and murder

[1422] Article 16 The South and the North shall not engage in propaganda or agitation activities aimed at destroying or subverting the other side

[1423] Article 17 The South and the North shall not form, support or protect terrorist groups or organizations that aim to destroy or overthrow the other side's system and political order in their respective regions, the other side's regions or overseas

[1424] Chapter 5 Transition of the Armistice State into a State of Peace Article 18 In order to transform the current state of armistice into a solid state of peace between the South and the North, the South and the North shall establish the 'Agreement on Non-Aggression and Exchanges and Cooperation' and the 'Agreement on the Denuclearization of the Korean Peninsula'. Article 19 The South and the North shall devise appropriate measures to transform the current state of armistice into a solid state of peace between the South and the North

[1425] Article 120: The South and the North shall faithfully observe the current Military Ceasefire Agreement until a solid state of peace is established between the South and the North

[1426] Chapter 6 Cooperation on the International Stage Article 21 The South and the North shall not slander or defame each other on the international stage, including at international organizations or conferences, and shall cooperate closely to protect the dignity of the nation

[1427] Article 3 The South and the North shall respect each other's interests on the international stage, hold close consultations on issues related to the interests of the people, and seek necessary measures

[1428] Article 36. In order to implement the agreements on inter-Korean reconciliation in Chapter 1 of the "Agreement on Reconciliation, Non-Aggression, and Exchanges and Cooperation between South and North Korea," a separate agreement on the establishment and operation of the "Joint Committee for Inter-Korean Reconciliation" will be created. Within the "Joint Committee for Inter-Korean Reconciliation," a "Legal Working Council" and a "Working Council for Stopping Defamation and Libel" will be established

[1435] Chapter 1 Non-use of Force prohibits the use of force in any form, including shooting, shelling, bombing, stabbing, and destruction, against the opposing side's personnel, materials, vehicles, and aircraft outside the area under one's jurisdiction, including the Military Demarcation Line, and refrain from any armed provocation that causes damage to the opposing side

[1436] Do not invade or attack the other party's jurisdiction or take any action against it, even temporarily, in whole or in part

[1437] The South and the North shall not allow regular military forces to invade each other's administrative areas by any means or method

[1438] The North shall not engage in any hostile acts of attacking, simulating attacks on, or obstructing the passage of personnel, materials, or means of transport between the South and the North in accordance with the agreement of both sides

[1439] The issue of not increasing military force in the area around the Military Demarcation Line raised by the North and South, the issue of not blockading the territorial waters and airspace of the other side for reconnaissance activities, and the issue of the Seoul and Pyongyang areas raised by the South will continue to be discussed at the South-North Joint Military Committee

[1440] Chapter 2 Peaceful Resolution of Disputes and Prevention of Accidental Armed Conflicts If any sign of armed invasion by the other party is discovered, which is deemed to be planned, the other party may immediately request an explanation and take necessary precautionary measures to prevent it from escalating into an armed conflict

[1441] In the event that an accidental armed conflict or the possibility of an accident is discovered due to misunderstanding, misunderstanding, mistake or unavoidable accident of both sides, both sides shall immediately notify the other side in accordance with the signal regulations agreed upon by both sides and establish prior measures to protect the name

[1442] Article 5. If an armed group or individual person, vehicle, vessel, warship, or aircraft of either side invades the other side's maritime zone due to a natural disaster, an unavoidable circumstance, or other reasons such as the failure of a military unit or air route between the South and the North, the invading side shall immediately notify the other side that it has no hostile intent and follow the other side's instructions, and the other side shall take measures to ensure the evacuation of the invading side as soon as possible

[1443] The period of return shall be within 1 year, and the authorities shall decide on the military affairs

[1444] It may take longer than that

[1445] In the event of a conflict such as an accidental intrusion or an accidental armed clash, both sides shall immediately cease hostile acts by their armed groups and promptly discuss all opinions and disputes in all fields, including the military hotline, through mechanisms involving the military authorities of both sides. If either side violates this agreement for the implementation and observance of non-aggression, the two sides shall conduct a joint investigation. - 224 - --- Page

[1446] We will determine responsibility for violations and take measures to prevent recurrence

[1447] Chapter 3 Non-aggression boundary line and zone Article 9 The non-aggression boundary line and zone on land between the South and the North shall be the Military Demarcation Line stipulated in the Military Armistice Agreement and the zones that have been under the jurisdiction of both sides until now

[1448] Article 10: The maritime non-aggression boundary between the South and the North will continue to be negotiated in the future

[1449] The maritime non-aggression zone shall be the area that both sides have been observing until the maritime non-aggression boundary is established

[1450] Article 11 The air non-aggression boundary and zone between the South and the North shall be the land and sea non-aggression boundary and the airspace above the jurisdictional zone

[1451] Chapter 4 Establishment and Operation of Military Hotline In order to prevent accidental armed conflict and its escalation, the South and the North shall establish and operate a military hotline between the Minister of National Defense of the South and the Minister of the People's Armed Forces of the North

[1452] Article 18 The operation of the military hotline shall be by means of communication implied by both sides, either by sending documents or exchanging telegrams. If necessary, the military authorities of both sides may communicate directly

[1453] Technical and practical issues arising in connection with the installation and operation of military direct telephones shall be resolved promptly after this memorandum comes into effect through contact between five communication specialists from each side of the South and the North

When 417 joins, it becomes mandatory to conclude a safeguards agreement with 141 Progress of promotion In 1958, Ireland submitted a resolution on the risk of nuclear weapons proliferation to the UN General Assembly. In 1966, the UN resolved to have the 18-nation disarmament conference establish treaty principles. It entered into force in 1970 under the leadership of the US, the Soviet Union, the UK, etc. Status of membership 187 countries (as of September 2000) ※ Major non-participating countries: Israel, India, Pakistan, Cuba, etc. South Korea joined in 1975, North Korea joined in 1985 (withdrawal declared in March 1993, withdrawal postponed in June 1993) Recent trends ＊ 417 Review/Extension Conference held in May 1995, decision made to extend indefinitely [Review conference held every 5 years] Advanced nuclear powers continue to insist on strengthening 477, while underdeveloped nuclear powers call for cooperation from advanced nuclear powers regarding passive security guarantees and peaceful use of nuclear energy. Comprehensive Nuclear-Test-Ban Treaty (21861: 60000「

[1546] Ban on all nuclear tests, including peaceful nuclear explosions, in all areas including underground, underwater, atmosphere, and space ㆍ Establishment of verification implementation system (international verification body, construction of nuclear test detection base network, etc.) Main contents ※ 44 countries including 5 nuclear weapon states, South and North Korea, India, Pakistan, and Israel (countries with nuclear power plants) Enter into force 180 days after ratification (however, cannot enter into force within 2 years) ㆍ ㆍ Since 1970, despite the adoption of several (7137 recommended resolutions) by the 1st Committee of the 1st Band (politics and security sector), no progress was made due to non-cooperation by nuclear powers such as the United States. Progress ㆍ End of the Cold War, conclusion of 87402' (Strategic Arms Reduction Treaty), increased necessity of concluding 07087 in 1995 due to [77 extended conference, etc. ㆍ In September 1996, led by Australia, Korea, the United States, Russia, etc. 126 countries jointly proposed ("Resolution 1087 submitted to the 52nd General Assembly 1044, adopted) Status of membership ㆍ After opening for signature in September

[1547] As of September, 155 countries including South Korea, the US, Japan, China, and Russia have signed (28 out of 44 required ratification countries have ratified), and 55 countries have ratified. Since March 1997, the signatories have been promoting the establishment of (Comprehensive Nuclear-Test-Ban Treaty Organization), which will mainly operate inspection, education, and surveillance systems. [1569] Signed in Washington on November

[1570] The Parties to this Treaty, recognizing their desire to live in peace with all peoples and all governments, and desiring to consolidate the framework of peace in the Pacific area, and desiring to declare publicly and formally their common determination to defend themselves against external armed attack so that no aggressor may be under the illusion that either of them is isolated in the Pacific area, and desiring to strengthen their efforts for collective defense to maintain peace and security in the Pacific area until a more comprehensive and effective regional security organization can emerge, do imply the following

[1571] Article 1 The Parties undertake to settle any international disputes in which they may be involved by peaceful means in such a manner that international peace and security and justice are not endangered and to refrain in their international relations from the threat or use of force in any manner inconsistent with the Purposes of the United Nations or obligations assumed by any Party toward the United Nations

[1572] Article 2 Whenever either Party considers that the political independence or stability of either Party is threatened by armed aggression from outside, the Parties shall engage in mutual assistance

[1573] The Parties will continue to strengthen, individually and jointly, through self-help and mutual assistance, appropriate means of deterring armed attack and will take appropriate steps, both proactive and implicit, to carry out this Treaty and further its purposes

[1574] Article 3 Each Party recognizes that an armed attack in the Pacific area against any Party in territory now under the administrative control of the other Party, or which it hereafter recognizes as lawfully brought under the administrative control of the other Party, would be dangerous to its own peace and security and declares that it will act in accordance with its constitutional procedures to address this common constitutionality

[1575] The Republic of Korea grants, and the United States of America accepts, the right to dispose United States land, sea, and air forces within and about the territory of the Republic of Korea as determined by mutual agreement

[1576] Article 5 This Treaty shall be ratified by the Republic of Korea and the United States of America in accordance with their respective constitutional procedures and shall come into force when the instruments of ratification thereof have been exchanged at Washington by the two countries

[1577] Article 6 This Treaty shall remain in force indefinitely

[1578] Either Party may terminate this Treaty one year after the other Party has notified the other Party of its intention to terminate it

[1579] IN WITNESS WHEREOF the undersigned Plenipotentiaries have signed this Treaty

[1580] Done at Washington, D.C., this first day of October 1958, in the Korean and English languages, for the Republic of Korea Byun Young-tae For the United States of America John Foster Delles - 238 - --- Page 239 --- 31st 50\ Joint Statement
